    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F32.2, F33.1, F33.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hello. Um... well, I suppose I'm here because I've been having some thoughts lately. It's a bit difficult to say out loud. \n\nI just... sometimes I catch myself thinking that it would be simpler if I just didn't wake up tomorrow. I'm not going to do anything, I don't have a plan to harm myself or anything like that. But... just having the thought scared me enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these thoughts and feelings?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose it's been about eight months. Give or take. That was when... well, my brother's health and his behavior changed very suddenly. It's just been going on since around then.\n\nDoctor: How often have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up? Are they happening every day, most days, or just occasionally?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not entirely sure. I haven't really kept track of it. Just... sometimes, I suppose. I don't think it's every day.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how often these thoughts about not wanting to wake up occur? For example, do they happen several times a week, once a week, or less often?\n\nPatient: Probably less often. I... I really don't keep a tally. When it crosses my mind, I just try to move past it, so... it's hard to put a number on it. I just know it happens sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don't enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you've lost interest in activities you once found pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I suppose so. I... I still set out my watercolor things sometimes. But I usually just sit there staring at the blank paper for a while... and then I end up putting it all away. The pleasure I used to feel in the color and the light, it just... it doesn't seem to arrive anymore. So I usually just stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, or feeling like you're not worth much?\n\nPatient: I suppose... a little. I don't really know about feeling worthless, but... I do feel guilty sometimes. Mostly about my brother. \n\nI just... I keep going back over his decline in my head. I tell myself that if I had been a better sister, or... just more attentive... I would have noticed sooner. Maybe I could have prevented some of it. But... I don't really like to dwell on that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most of the time, you feel down or sad, or is it more that you just don't get pleasure out of things like you used to?\n\nPatient: I suppose... maybe both. I don't know if I'd call it sad, exactly. It's more that... most days, it just feels like a gray film has settled over everything. \n\nEven when I'm just doing normal things... making tea, or looking out at my garden... there's this quiet heaviness in me. I try to reason my way out of it, but... I can't quite seem to. It just... stays.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or lacking in energy most days, even if you haven't done anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: No, I don't believe so. I... I haven't really noticed feeling tired. My energy seems about the same as it usually is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you've been moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or that you can't get interested in doing things you normally would?\n\nPatient: I haven't noticed any slowness, no. As for the other part... I suppose so. \n\nI still set out my watercolor things sometimes. But... I'll just sit there staring at the paper, and eventually I just put it all away. The... the pleasure I used to feel in the color and light, it just doesn't seem to arrive.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately—like having trouble falling or staying asleep, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I suppose my sleep hasn't been very good. I seem to fall asleep rather... unevenly. \n\nAnd then I tend to wake up around three or four in the morning. I just lie there, thinking about things. Phone calls I need to make, forms to fill out... wondering if I handled certain situations correctly. It’s just... quiet worry, I suppose. \n\nBy the time morning actually comes, I feel as if I've hardly rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way — with the gray film and heaviness — for most of the time over the last two years?\n\nPatient: No... not two years. I suppose it's been... about eight months. \n\nIt started around the time my brother... well, his health and behavior changed rather suddenly. Just since then, really.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you take pleasure in nothing these days, or that most activities don't bring you any enjoyment at all?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say nothing. No. I suppose... there are still things I enjoy well enough. \n\nIt's not everything. I haven't really thought about it in those absolute terms... it just depends on the day, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to focus or concentrate on tasks lately, like following a conversation or making decisions?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I can still follow along well enough... I mean, I can hold a conversation. I haven't really noticed any major issues with making decisions or anything like that. It's just the usual, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently low or that you've felt this heaviness for at least two years, even if there were times it felt a little better?\n\nPatient: No... I believe I just mentioned this. It hasn't been two years. Just the eight months or so, since my brother's situation. I didn't feel this way before that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently low or that you've felt this heaviness for at least two years, even if there were times it felt a little better?\n\nPatient: I'm sorry, didn't you just ask me that? As I said... no. It hasn't been two years. Just the eight months.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently low or that you've felt this heaviness for at least two years, even if there were times it felt a little better?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't understand why you keep asking me this. I've told you twice now. It hasn't been two years. It's been eight months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I... I still set out my watercolor things sometimes. But I usually just sit there staring at the blank paper for a while... and then I end up putting it all away. The pleasure I used to feel in the color and the light, it just... it doesn't seem to arrive anymore. So I usually just stop.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or blaming yourself for things more than usual, or feeling like you're not worth much?\n\nPatient: I suppose... a little. I don't really know about feeling worthless, but... I do feel guilty sometimes. Mostly about my brother. \n\nI just... I keep going back over his decline in my head. I tell myself that if I had been a better sister, or... just more attentive... I would have noticed sooner. Maybe I could have prevented some of it. But... I don't really like to dwell on that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most of the time, you feel down or sad, or is it more that you just don't get pleasure out of things like you used to?\n\nPatient: I suppose... maybe both. I don't know if I'd call it sad, exactly. It's more that... most days, it just feels like a gray film has settled over everything. \n\nEven when I'm just doing normal things... making tea, or looking out at my garden... there's this quiet heaviness in me. I try to reason my way out of it, but... I can't quite seem to. It just... stays.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel tired or lacking in energy most days, even if you haven't done anything physically demanding?\n\nPatient: No, I don't believe so. I... I haven't really noticed feeling tired. My energy seems about the same as it usually is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you've been moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or that you can't get interested in doing things you normally would?\n\nPatient: I haven't noticed any slowness, no. As for the other part... I suppose so. \n\nI still set out my watercolor things sometimes. But... I'll just sit there staring at the paper, and eventually I just put it all away. The... the pleasure I used to feel in the color and light, it just doesn't seem to arrive.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately—like having trouble falling or staying asleep, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I suppose my sleep hasn't been very good. I seem to fall asleep rather... unevenly. \n\nAnd then I tend to wake up around three or four in the morning. I just lie there, thinking about things. Phone calls I need to make, forms to fill out... wondering if I handled certain situations correctly. It’s just... quiet worry, I suppose. \n\nBy the time morning actually comes, I feel as if I've hardly rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt this way — with the gray film and heaviness — for most of the time over the last two years?\n\nPatient: No... not two years. I suppose it's been... about eight months. \n\nIt started around the time my brother... well, his health and behavior changed rather suddenly. Just since then, really.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you take pleasure in nothing these days, or that most activities don't bring you any enjoyment at all?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say nothing. No. I suppose... there are still things I enjoy well enough. \n\nIt's not everything. I haven't really thought about it in those absolute terms... it just depends on the day, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that it's harder to focus or concentrate on tasks lately, like following a conversation or making decisions?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I can still follow along well enough... I mean, I can hold a conversation. I haven't really noticed any major issues with making decisions or anything like that. It's just the usual, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently low or that you've felt this heaviness for at least two years, even if there were times it felt a little better?\n\nPatient: No... I believe I just mentioned this. It hasn't been two years. Just the eight months or so, since my brother's situation. I didn't feel this way before that.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently low or that you've felt this heaviness for at least two years, even if there were times it felt a little better?\n\nPatient: I'm sorry, didn't you just ask me that? As I said... no. It hasn't been two years. Just the eight months.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been consistently low or that you've felt this heaviness for at least two years, even if there were times it felt a little better?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't understand why you keep asking me this. I've told you twice now. It hasn't been two years. It's been eight months.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you take pleasure in almost nothing these days, or that most activities don’t bring you any enjoyment at all?\n\nPatient: Well... I suppose so. I still set out my watercolor things sometimes. But I'll just... sit there, staring at the paper. Then I put it all away. The pleasure I used to feel in the color and the light... it just doesn't seem to arrive anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you take pleasure in almost nothing these days, or that most activities don’t bring you any enjoyment at all?\n\nPatient: I... didn't I just answer this? I told you about my watercolors. I really don't have anything else to say about it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
